Reproducing Didion-Gency et al. (2024) from Dryad dataset 4j0zpc8hz¶

Paper. Didion-Gency M, Vitasse Y, Buchmann N, Gessler A, Gisler J, Schaub M, Grossiord C (2024). Chronic warming and dry soils limit carbon uptake and growth despite a longer growing season in beech and oak. Plant Physiology 194, doi:10.1093/plphys/kiad565. The claims file was written from the version of record in Europe PMC (PMC10828195) with its supplement (Supplemental Figures S1 to S7, Tables S1 to S6, Methods S1 to S4); no figure was seen.

Dataset. doi:10.5061/dryad.4j0zpc8hz, version id 260590 in Dryad's API (version number 3, published 2023-10-17). The paper cites the dataset's DOI, which resolves to the latest version, and no version. Dryad lists only this one version, so it is pinned. Files read, with the SHA-256 that Dryad's file list gives and that the first cell checks:

File SHA-256
Dryad_Data_PlantPhy_MargauxDidionGency.xlsx b18a8e25527e2f5ecc80a9d520913b38eafb4382fe7e6c5e8ee7232e243300a3
Metadata.csv eaffc99d0e0a23c7e3bab236d4ecd951c92733cacbf9d7c8a57447a9b5ad0766

The dataset's README.md (the same text as the workbook's README sheet) is the third file of the version; the analysis does not use it. Dryad needs a token to download, so the files are staged before the notebook runs and read in place (docs/archives.md, "Dryad").

What the dataset holds. One table, one row per tree and measurement campaign: 96 trees (beech and oak, monocultures and mixtures, 6 per treatment, species and mixture, in 16 chambers), 3 campaigns a year in 2019 to 2021, with the yearly phenology, the treatment's mean air temperature per season, GDD and chilling days per tree, the leaf-level traits per campaign (Asat, sugar, starch, predawn and midday water potential) and the yearly height, diameter and AGWB increments. It has no soil moisture, no chamber climate records and no initial tree sizes.

Outside inputs. None. AGWB increments are in the dataset, so the coefficients of Annighofer et al. (2016) are not needed.

Readings. Where the claims file leaves a detail open it names readings (P1/P2, T1/T2, S1/S2/S3, L1/L2, I1/I2, Y1/Y2, B1/B2, and for Table S6 y1/y2 and F1/F2). Every reading is run. A first run of this attempt asked three questions (questions/dryad-4j0zpc8hz.yaml); a reader answered them from the paper and the claims file was updated, and this version of the notebook follows the updated file. The paper does not say how negative values were square-rooted, so negative starch, sugar and Asat values are read as negative increments are, under S1 (untransformed), S2 (square root after adding the constant that brings the minimum to zero) and a new reading S3 (negative values dropped, as R's sqrt gives NaN for them). The interaction model takes the years of reading Y1 (bud swelling and development duration on 2020 and 2021, the other traits on 2019 to 2021, as the F and p pairs of Tables S2 and S3 imply), which the criteria use, with Y2 (2020 and 2021 for all traits) as a variant. The Table S6 model is run on 2019 to 2021 (y1) and on 2020 and 2021 (y2), with sequential (F1) and marginal (F2) F tests, all of which are readings of its criterion.

After the first verification round, three more questions were asked (questions/dryad-4j0zpc8hz.yaml): over which family the mixture against monoculture contrasts are adjusted, run here as A1 (each contrast alone) and A2 (single-step over the contrasts of the model); whether Eqs. 3 and 4 are to be read so that the complementarity effect is twice the NBE and the selection effect is 0 under B1; and whether growing season length is the deposited Growth_length or bud swelling to onset of senescence. Two variants that are not readings of the claims file are also shown: the regressions of reading P1 with a random intercept per treatment and year, and the Table S6 F values set against the paper's.

In [1]:
import time
import warnings

import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
import numpy as np
import pandas as pd

import oaktrees as o

pd.set_option("display.width", 220)
pd.set_option("display.max_columns", 40)
pd.set_option("display.max_rows", 400)
warnings.filterwarnings("ignore")
started = time.time()

print(f"Dryad dataset {o.DOI}, version id {o.VERSION_ID}; files read in place from {o.data_dir()}")
print(o.check_files().to_string(index=False))
workbook = o.read_workbook()
metadata = o.read_metadata()
raw = workbook["Total Data"]
d = o.load(raw)
ty = o.tree_year_table(d)          # one row per tree and year (leaf-level traits: yearly tree means, L1)
ca = o.campaign_table(d)           # one row per tree and campaign (L2)
print(f"loaded in {time.time() - started:.1f} s")

Data check¶

Each check of the description against the values is stated under its output. The dataset has no time series (no chamber climate records), so there is no clock to align; the only time variables are the year and the campaign number.

The metadata name the treatments C, W, MR and WMR; the data use C, D, H and HD. The Warming and Soil moisture reduction columns map them one to one: H is warming (W), D is moisture reduction (MR) and HD both (WMR). The analysis uses the paper's names, from those two columns. 35 rows repeat another row exactly (trees 17, 35, 45 and 144). They are counted once. Tree 144 also has two different rows for campaign 5 of 2020 (same Asat and water potentials, different sugar and starch), which are averaged. The design matches the paper: 96 trees, 6 per treatment, species and mixture, 4 chambers per treatment, 3 campaigns a year in 2019 to 2021.

Ranges and units are plausible for the phenology dates (days of year 64 to 291) and durations. The air temperatures are one value per treatment and year, the same in the four chambers of a treatment: the dataset gives no chamber climate, and the sensor height (50 cm or 2 m) is not stated. Starch (77 rows), sugar (3) and Asat (5) have negative values, which a concentration or a light-saturated assimilation rate cannot have. Height increments are negative in 30 rows and diameter increments in 90, which a shrinking stem can give only within measurement error. Soil moisture, the chamber climate records (daily air temperature, VPD, soil water content), the initial heights and diameters (Table S4) and the absolute heights, diameters and AGWB are not in the dataset.

How the dataset describes the derived variables (Metadata.csv; the workbook's README sheet):
  Growth_length [days]: Growing season length
                 README sheet: Growing season length
  GDD [°C days]: Growing degree day requirement (i.e., accumulated the daily mean temperature above a threshold of 5°C from January 1st to the date of bud swelling)
       README sheet: Growing degree day requirement (i.e., accumulated the daily mean temperature above a threshold of 5°C from January 1st to the date of bud swelling)
  Chill_days [days]: Chilling days (i.e., days with average temperature somewhat below 5-10°C from the November 1st to the date of leaf flushing) 
              README sheet: Chilling days (i.e., days with average temperature somewhat below 5-10°C from the November 1st to the date of leaf flushing)

AGWB increment exactly 0 in 57 of 191 tree-years 2020-21; their height increments: {'count': 57.0, 'mean': 7.2, 'std': 14.9, 'min': -12.5, '25%': 0.0, '50%': 5.0, '75%': 10.0, 'max': 103.0}

Growth_length never equals onset of senescence minus bud swelling, the Methods' definition of growing season length; it is close to the end of senescence (10% date plus senescence duration) minus bud swelling, but not equal to it either. The deposited Growth_length is used, as the claims file names the dataset's growing season length; the Methods' definition is shown as a variant in fig1ij. The onset of senescence sits at day 250 to 252 in many tree-years: the observations began in September, so these values are censored at the first observation. Diameter increments of a median of about 0.5 "cm" a year on stems of about 6 mm are implausible; the values are consistent with millimetres. Some compartments hold a monoculture tree of one species and a mixture tree of the other, which the design (each compartment a monoculture or a mixture) does not allow; the compartment labels are not used. AGWB increments are 0 in many tree-years whose height increment is positive.

The deposited data against the paper's Table S1¶

Table S1 gives F tests of the treatment model. This check refits it on 2019 to 2021 (as the table does), on yearly tree means (L1) and on campaign values (L2), and gives both the sequential tests (the claims file's reading) and the marginal ones, for traits whose values have no negatives, where the square root is defined. It is a check of the data and of the model code, and decides no outcome.

In [5]:
TABLE_S1 = {   # Supplemental Table S1, F values in the order W, MR, Y, W:MR, W:Y, MR:Y, W:MR:Y
    ("B", "Photo"): [2.44, 0.99, 25.86, 4.12, 5.22, 19.00, 2.75],
    ("O", "Photo"): [0.00, 2.70, 3.41, 0.17, 1.02, 0.66, 1.58],
    ("B", "AGB_growth"): [0.17, 0.04, 1.75, 0.09, 0.19, 2.83, 1.08],
    ("O", "AGB_growth"): [0.33, 0.93, 5.05, 0.89, 1.62, 7.27, 1.04],
    ("B", "Predawn"): [2.83, 0.10, 0.25, 0.00, 2.12, 0.12, 3.54],
    ("O", "Predawn"): [0.34, 0.07, 7.95, 2.34, 0.90, 1.70, 3.59],
    ("B", "Midday"): [0.96, 0.06, 6.50, 0.04, 2.10, 0.27, 0.97],
    ("O", "Midday"): [0.50, 0.04, 2.25, 0.00, 0.35, 0.95, 0.42],
}
rows = []
for (sp, trait), paper in TABLE_S1.items():
    sq = trait not in ("Predawn", "Midday")
    for L, data in (("L1", ty), ("L2", ca)):
        r = o.treatment_test(data, sp, trait, [2019, 2020, 2021], sq, "S1")
        for kind in ("anova", "anova_marginal"):
            F = r[kind].F.round(2).tolist()
            rows.append({"species": sp, "trait": trait, "L": L, "tests": "sequential" if kind == "anova" else "marginal",
                         "n": r["n"], "F": F, "max_abs_diff": float(np.max(np.abs(np.array(F) - paper)))})
link = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(link.to_string(index=False))
best = link.loc[link.groupby(["species", "trait"]).max_abs_diff.idxmin()]
print("\nClosest version per trait:")
print(best[["species", "trait", "L", "tests", "max_abs_diff"]].to_string(index=False))

      B      Photo L1 sequential  72   [0.75, 34.0, 9.59, 0.47, 2.29, 16.74, 1.99]         33.01
      B      Photo L1   marginal  72  [2.26, 1.03, 18.97, 3.29, 3.95, 15.05, 1.99]          6.89
      B      Photo L2 sequential 215  [0.86, 34.03, 13.1, 0.78, 2.86, 19.99, 2.74]         33.04
      B      Photo L2   marginal 215    [2.43, 1.0, 25.72, 4.11, 5.2, 18.75, 2.74]          0.25

The marginal tests on the deposited data reproduce Table S1 for oak AGWB increment exactly (largest difference in F 0.00, yearly tree means), for beech Asat closely (0.25, campaign values) and for oak midday water potential within 0.02 (campaign values). The sequential tests, which the claims file names, differ from the table by up to 68 in F. Other traits match less well. This is consistent with the deposited data being the paper's data and with the paper's F tests being marginal; it decides no outcome, and the question is asked (questions/dryad-4j0zpc8hz.yaml). The criteria below rest on the contrasts, which do not depend on how the F tests are taken.

Phenology regressions (Figure 1)¶

Phenology regression of the claims file: per species, monoculture trees, 2020 and 2021 pooled, a linear regression of the trait on the season's air temperature. Reading P1 is each tree and year against the temperature of its chamber; the dataset gives the temperature per treatment and year only, so P1 here uses the treatment's value (the same in its four chambers). Reading P2 is the eight treatment and year means. The sensor readings H1 and H2 cannot be told apart: the dataset gives one temperature and does not say which sensor it comes from.

In [6]:
import statsmodels.formula.api as smf

mono = ty[(ty.Mixture == "Monoculture") & ty.Year.isin([2020, 2021])]

def pheno(trait, temp):
    rows = []
    for sp in ("B", "O"):
        s = mono[mono.Species == sp].dropna(subset=[trait, temp])
        r1 = o.linear_fit(s[temp], s[trait])
        m = s.groupby(["treat", "Year"])[[trait, temp]].mean()
        r2 = o.linear_fit(m[temp], m[trait])
        rows += [{"species": sp, "reading": "P1", **r1}, {"species": sp, "reading": "P2", **r2}]
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

def within(v, target, tol):
    return abs(v - target) <= tol + 1e-12

def judge_slopes(tab, targets):
    out = []
    for reading, g in tab.groupby("reading"):
        g = g.set_index("species")
        ok = all(within(g.loc[sp, "slope"], targets[sp][0], targets[sp][1]) and g.loc[sp, "p"] <= 0.05 for sp in "BO")
        out.append({"reading": reading, "slopes": g.slope.round(3).to_dict(), "p": g.p.map(lambda v: f"{v:.2g}").to_dict(),
                    "all_within_and_p<=0.05": ok})
    return pd.DataFrame(out)

def pheno_re(trait, temp):
    """Variant of P1 (not a reading of the claims file): each tree-year, with a random intercept per treatment
    and year, as the temperature is one value per treatment and year (REML, Wald z of the slope)."""
    rows = []
    for sp in ("B", "O"):
        s = mono[mono.Species == sp].dropna(subset=[trait, temp]).assign(g=lambda f: f.treat + f.Year.astype(str))
        f = smf.mixedlm(f"{trait} ~ {temp}", s, groups=s.g).fit(reml=True)
        rows.append({"species": sp, "reading": "P1 + random treatment-year", "slope": f.params[temp], "se": f.bse[temp],
                     "p": f.pvalues[temp], "n": len(s), "groups": s.g.nunique(), "converged": f.converged})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

PH, PHRE = {}, {}
for cid, trait, temp in [("fig1ab", "Flush_S1", "Tspring"), ("fig1cd", "Dev_time", "Tspring"), ("fig1ef", "Senes_10", "Tfall"),
                         ("fig1gh", "Senes_time", "Tfall"), ("fig1ij", "Growth_length", "Tannual")]:
    PH[cid] = pheno(trait, temp)
    PHRE[cid] = pheno_re(trait, temp)
print("Monoculture tree-years with data, 2020-21:", mono.groupby("Species")[["Flush_S1", "Dev_time", "Senes_10", "Senes_time", "Growth_length"]].count().to_dict("index"))
print("Temperature ranges (deg C):", {t: (round(mono[t].min(), 2), round(mono[t].max(), 2)) for t in ("Tspring", "Tfall", "Tannual")})

Monoculture tree-years with data, 2020-21: {'B': {'Flush_S1': 48, 'Dev_time': 48, 'Senes_10': 48, 'Senes_time': 48, 'Growth_length': 48}, 'O': {'Flush_S1': 48, 'Dev_time': 48, 'Senes_10': 48, 'Senes_time': 48, 'Growth_length': 48}}
Temperature ranges (deg C): {'Tspring': (np.float64(6.68), np.float64(14.25)), 'Tfall': (np.float64(8.62), np.float64(13.98)), 'Tannual': (np.float64(10.47), np.float64(16.9))}

fig1ab-bud-swelling-advance¶

Warmer spring air advanced bud swelling in both species, by 1.1 days per degree in beech and 2.3 days per degree in oak, across 2020 and 2021 and the four treatments.

fig1-oak-steeper-advance¶

The temperature-driven advance was larger in oak than in beech (P = 0.004, least squares analysis).

In [11]:
rows = []
for reading, trait, temp in [("a", "Flush_S1", "Tspring"), ("b", "Growth_length", "Tannual")]:
    s = mono.dropna(subset=[trait])[["Species", "treat", "Year", trait, temp]]
    for unit, data in (("P1", s), ("P2", s.groupby(["Species", "treat", "Year"], as_index=False)[[trait, temp]].mean())):
        fit = smf.ols(f"{trait} ~ {temp} * C(Species)", data).fit()
        term = f"{temp}:C(Species)[T.O]"
        slope_b = fit.params[temp]
        slope_o = slope_b + fit.params[term]
        rows.append({"reading": reading, "unit": unit, "slope_beech": slope_b, "slope_oak": slope_o,
                     "interaction_p": fit.pvalues[term], "n": int(fit.nobs),
                     "oak_larger_and_p<0.05": bool(abs(slope_o) > abs(slope_b) and fit.pvalues[term] < 0.05)})
steep = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(steep.round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for reading, trait, temp in [("a", "Flush_S1", "Tspring"), ("b", "Growth_length", "Tannual")]:
    s = mono.dropna(subset=[trait]).assign(g=lambda f: f.Species + f.treat + f.Year.astype(str))
    fit = smf.mixedlm(f"{trait} ~ {temp} * C(Species)", s, groups=s.g).fit(reml=True)
    term = f"{temp}:C(Species)[T.O]"
    rows.append({"reading": reading, "unit": "P1 + random species-treatment-year", "slope_beech": fit.params[temp],
                 "slope_oak": fit.params[temp] + fit.params[term], "interaction_p": fit.pvalues[term], "n": len(s),
                 "groups": s.g.nunique(), "converged": fit.converged})
print("\nVariant, P1 with a random intercept per species, treatment and year (Wald z):")
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).round(4).to_string(index=False))

fig2-gdd-chilling¶

The more chilling days trees received, the fewer growing degree days they needed to reach bud swelling, in both species, following a negative exponential.

The dataset gives GDD and chilling days per tree and year, computed by the authors; the chamber temperature records they come from are not in it. So the claims file's readings of how they are computed ((a) or (b) for GDD, (c) or (d) for the end of the chilling count, sensor H1 or H2) cannot be run one by one: the test uses the deposited values, whose description says chilling days are counted to leaf flushing (reading d) and GDD accumulate "the daily mean temperature above a threshold of 5 C" (a or b). Both forms of the negative exponential, (e) and (f), are fitted.

In [13]:
rows = []
for sp in "BO":
    s = mono[mono.Species == sp].dropna(subset=["GDD", "Chill_days"])
    for form in ("e", "f"):
        r = o.nls_exponential(s.Chill_days, s.GDD, form)
        rows.append({"species": sp, **{k: r.get(k) for k in ("form", "n", "converged", "a", "b", "c", "rate", "p_rate", "r2", "decreasing")}})
gdd = pd.DataFrame(rows)
gdd["holds"] = gdd.converged & gdd.decreasing & (gdd.p_rate <= 0.05)
print(gdd.to_string(index=False))
print("Ranges: Chill_days", mono.Chill_days.min(), "to", mono.Chill_days.max(), "; GDD", round(mono.GDD.min(), 1), "to", round(mono.GDD.max(), 1))
print("Spearman correlation of GDD with chilling days:",
      {sp: round(mono[mono.Species == sp][["GDD", "Chill_days"]].corr("spearman").iloc[0, 1], 3) for sp in "BO"})

Treatment tests (Figures 3 and 4)¶

Treatment test of the claims file: per species, monoculture trees, lme(trait ~ (W + MR + year)^3, random = ~1|Chamber), fitted by REML; Tukey-type contrasts among the four treatments within each year, with single-step adjusted p over the six pairs of a year. Square root for starch, sugar, Asat and the increments, none for the water potentials. Readings: T1 (2019 to 2021) and T2 (2020 and 2021); where some values to be square-rooted are negative (starch, sugar and Asat as well as the increments), S1 (untransformed), S2 (square root after adding the constant that brings the minimum to zero) and S3 (negative values dropped, the others square-rooted); L1 (yearly tree means) and L2 (campaign values) for starch, sugar and Asat. A contrast is significant at adjusted p < 0.05. In the tables, raw_diff is the difference of the raw means (first treatment minus second) of the rows the model uses (under S3, without the dropped rows) and estimate the model contrast on the transformed scale. The water potentials are not transformed, so S1, S2 and S3 give the same fit for them.

In [15]:
LEAF_C = ["Starch", "Sugar", "Photo"]
GROWTH = ["Height_growth", "Diameter_growth", "AGB_growth"]
WP = ["Predawn", "Midday"]
YEARS = {"T1": [2019, 2020, 2021], "T2": [2020, 2021]}
SS = ("S1", "S2", "S3")
TT, fits = {}, []
t0 = time.time()
for sp in "BO":
    for trait in LEAF_C + GROWTH + WP:
        for T, yrs in YEARS.items():
            for L, data in (("L1", ty), ("L2", ca)):
                if L == "L2" and trait not in LEAF_C:
                    continue
                for S in SS:
                    r = o.treatment_test(data, sp, trait, yrs, trait not in WP, S)
                    TT[(sp, trait, T, S, L)] = r
                    fits.append({"species": sp, "trait": trait, "T": T, "L": L, "S": S, "n": r["n"],
                                 "transform": r["transform"], "converged": r["converged"]})
fits = pd.DataFrame(fits)
print(f"{len(fits)} fits in {time.time() - t0:.0f} s; not converged: {int((~fits.converged).sum())}")
print(fits.drop_duplicates(["species", "trait", "T", "L", "transform"]).to_string(index=False))
print("\nRows dropped under S3 (negative values), per species, trait, years and level:")
n12 = fits[fits.S != "S3"].groupby(["species", "trait", "T", "L"]).n.max()
n3 = fits[fits.S == "S3"].set_index(["species", "trait", "T", "L"]).n
print((n12 - n3).rename("dropped")[lambda s: s > 0].to_string())

def readings(trait):
    Ls = ("L1", "L2") if trait in LEAF_C else ("L1",)
    return [(T, S, L) for T in YEARS for S in SS for L in Ls]

def contrast(sp, trait, reading, year, label):
    T, S, L = reading
    c = TT[(sp, trait, T, S, L)]["contrasts"]
    return c[(c.year == year) & (c.contrast == label)].iloc[0]

def below(sp, trait, reading, year, label):
    r = contrast(sp, trait, reading, year, label)
    return bool(r.p_adj < 0.05 and r.estimate < 0)

def signif(sp, trait, reading, year, label):
    return bool(contrast(sp, trait, reading, year, label).p_adj < 0.05)

def table(sp, traits, label, years=(2020, 2021)):
    rows = []
    for trait in traits:
        for reading in readings(trait):
            for yr in years:
                r = contrast(sp, trait, reading, yr, label)
                rows.append({"species": sp, "trait": trait, "reading": "/".join(reading), "year": yr, "contrast": label,
                             "raw_diff": r.raw_diff, "estimate": r.estimate, "z": r.z, "p_adj": r.p_adj})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

print(pd.concat([table(sp, WP, "W-C", [2020]) for sp in "BO"]).round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for reading in readings("Predawn"):
    conds = {"PD beech": below("B", "Predawn", reading, 2020, "W-C"), "PD oak": below("O", "Predawn", reading, 2020, "W-C"),
             "MD beech": below("B", "Midday", reading, 2020, "W-C"), "MD oak not": not below("O", "Midday", reading, 2020, "W-C")}
    n = sum(conds.values())
    rows.append({"reading": "/".join(reading), **conds, "holding": n,
                 "outcome": "reproduced" if n == 4 else "partially_reproduced" if n >= 2 else "not_reproduced"})

disc-psi-minimum¶

Under the low moisture treatment trees reached predawn water potentials of about -1.9 MPa in beech and -1.2 MPa in oak.

In [19]:
m = ty[(ty.Mixture == "Monoculture") & ty.Year.isin([2020, 2021])]
mc = ca[(ca.Mixture == "Monoculture") & ca.Year.isin([2020, 2021])]
yearly = m.groupby(["Species", "treat", "Year"]).Predawn.mean()
camp = mc.groupby(["Species", "treat", "Year", "season"]).Predawn.mean()
rows = []
for sp, target, tol in (("B", -1.9, 0.19), ("O", -1.2, 0.12)):
    vals = {"a": yearly.loc[sp].loc[["MR"]].min(), "b": yearly.loc[sp].loc[["MR", "WMR"]].min(),
            "c": camp.loc[sp].loc[["MR", "WMR"]].min(), "d": mc[(mc.Species == sp) & mc.treat.isin(["MR", "WMR"])].Predawn.min()}
    for k, v in vals.items():
        rows.append({"species": sp, "reading": k, "value": v, "target": target, "within": within(v, target, tol)})
psi = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(psi.round(3).to_string(index=False))
print("\nReadings with both species within tolerance:",
      [k for k in "abcd" if psi[psi.reading == k].within.all()])
print("\nYearly treatment means (MPa):"); print(yearly.unstack([1]).round(3).to_string())

In beech, warming alone had no effect on starch, sugar, Asat, or height, diameter and AGWB increments.

Result: blocked (methods underspecified). Of 108 W against C contrasts in beech (six traits, two years, twelve readings), two are significant, both starch in 2020 under T1 on yearly means: under S2 (raw difference -3.52 %, adjusted p 0.041) and under S3 (p 0.018). Under S1 the same contrast has p 0.099, and under T2 or on campaign values p 0.050 to 0.33. The answer to the question named S1, S2 and S3 for the negative starch values; these readings still disagree, so the claim stays blocked. The warming main effect is not significant in the marginal tests under T1 (p 0.081 to 0.72).

fig4-oak-warming-growth-2021¶

In oak, warming alone reduced height, diameter and AGWB increments, in 2021 only.

t = table("O", GROWTH, "W-C")
print(t.round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for reading in readings("Height_growth"):
    n21 = sum(below("O", tr, reading, 2021, "W-C") for tr in GROWTH)
    n20 = sum(below("O", tr, reading, 2020, "W-C") for tr in GROWTH)
    out = ("reproduced" if n21 == 3 and n20 == 0 else "partially_reproduced" if n21 in (1, 2) and n20 == 0 else "not_reproduced")
    rows.append({"reading": "/".join(reading), "W<C in 2021": n21, "W<C in 2020": n20, "outcome": out})
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).to_string(index=False))

In beech, lower soil moisture alone reduced Asat in 2021, height increment in 2021, diameter increment in 2020 and 2021, and AGWB increment in 2021.

t = table("B", LEAF_C + GROWTH, "MR-C")
print(t.round(4).to_string(index=False))
named = [("Photo", 2021), ("Height_growth", 2021), ("Diameter_growth", 2020), ("Diameter_growth", 2021), ("AGB_growth", 2021)]
rows = []
for reading in [(T, S, L) for T in YEARS for S in SS for L in ("L1", "L2")]:
    hits = {f"{tr} {yr}": below("B", tr, reading if tr in LEAF_C else (reading[0], reading[1], "L1"), yr, "MR-C") for tr, yr in named}
    n = sum(hits.values())
    rows.append({"reading": "/".join(reading), **hits, "n": n,
                 "outcome": "reproduced" if n == 5 else "partially_reproduced" if n >= 3 else "not_reproduced"})
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).to_string(index=False))

fig4-oak-mr¶

In oak, lower soil moisture alone reduced Asat and height, diameter and AGWB increments in both 2020 and 2021.

In [24]:
t = table("O", ["Photo"] + GROWTH, "MR-C")
print(t.round(4).to_string(index=False))

def count_table(sp, label, trait_years, full, partial_min):
    rows = []
    for reading in [(T, S, L) for T in YEARS for S in SS for L in ("L1", "L2")]:
        hits = {f"{tr} {yr}": below(sp, tr, reading if tr in LEAF_C else (reading[0], reading[1], "L1"), yr, label)
                for tr, yr in trait_years}
        n = sum(hits.values())
        rows.append({"reading": "/".join(reading), **hits, "n": n,
                     "outcome": "reproduced" if n == full else "partially_reproduced" if n >= partial_min else "not_reproduced"})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

print(count_table("O", "MR-C", [(tr, yr) for tr in ["Photo"] + GROWTH for yr in (2020, 2021)], 8, 4).to_string(index=False))

disc-beech-asat-more-impaired¶

Low soil moisture impaired Asat more strongly in beech than in oak.

In [29]:
m = ty[(ty.Mixture == "Monoculture") & ty.Year.isin([2020, 2021])]
rows = []
for X in (["MR"], ["MR", "WMR"], ["WMR"]):
    for yrs in ([2020], [2021], [2020, 2021]):
        res = {}
        for sp in "BO":
            s = m[(m.Species == sp) & m.Year.isin(yrs)]
            c = s[s.treat == "C"].Photo.mean()
            x = s[s.treat.isin(X)].Photo.mean()
            res[sp] = (c, x)
        r1 = {sp: (c - x) / c for sp, (c, x) in res.items()}
        r2 = {sp: c - x for sp, (c, x) in res.items()}
        rows.append({"X": "+".join(X), "years": "+".join(map(str, yrs)), "C_beech": res["B"][0], "X_beech": res["B"][1],
                     "C_oak": res["O"][0], "X_oak": res["O"][1], "r1_beech": r1["B"], "r1_oak": r1["O"], "r2_beech": r2["B"],
                     "r2_oak": r2["O"], "r1_beech_larger": r1["B"] > r1["O"], "r2_beech_larger": r2["B"] > r2["O"]})
imp = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print(imp.round(3).to_string(index=False))
named = imp[imp.X != "WMR"]
print("\nReadings of the criterion (X = MR or MR+WMR): beech larger in",
      int(named.r1_beech_larger.sum() + named.r2_beech_larger.sum()), "of", 2 * len(named))

tables6-no-seasonal-variation¶

Asat, starch and sugar did not vary among the three campaigns of the growing season.

In [30]:
S6Y = {"y1": [2019, 2020, 2021], "y2": [2020, 2021]}
rows = []
for trait in LEAF_C:
    for Y, yrs in S6Y.items():
        for trees in ("mono", "mono+mix"):
            base = ca[ca.Year.isin(yrs) & ((ca.Mixture == "Monoculture") if trees == "mono" else True)]
            for group in ("pooled", "B", "O"):
                sub0 = (base if group == "pooled" else base[base.Species == group]).dropna(subset=[trait])
                for S in SS:
                    sub = sub0.copy()
                    sub["y"], how = o.transform(sub[trait], True, S)
                    sub = sub.dropna(subset=["y"])
                    mdl = o.LME(sub, "y", "(C(W) + C(M) + C(season))**3")
                    for F, a in (("F1", mdl.anova_sequential()), ("F2", mdl.anova_marginal())):
                        rows.append({"trait": trait, "Y": Y, "trees": trees, "species": group, "S": S, "F": F,
                                     "transform": how, "n": len(sub), "converged": mdl.converged,
                                     "season_F": a.F[2], "season_p": a.p[2], "WS_F": a.F[4], "MS_F": a.F[5], "WMS_F": a.F[6]})
s6 = pd.DataFrame(rows)
print("Fits not converged:", int((~s6.converged).sum()))
print(s6.round(4).to_string(index=False))
by = s6.groupby(["Y", "trees", "species", "S", "F"]).season_p.apply(lambda p: bool((p > 0.05).all())).rename("all_three_p_over_005")
print("\nSeason term p > 0.05 for all three traits, per group:")
print(by.unstack("F").to_string())
rd = []
for Y in S6Y:
    for trees in ("mono", "mono+mix"):
        for S in SS:
            for F in ("F1", "F2"):
                a = bool(by.loc[(Y, trees, "pooled", S, F)])
                b = bool(by.loc[(Y, trees, "B", S, F)] and by.loc[(Y, trees, "O", S, F)])
                rd += [{"species": "a (pooled)", "Y": Y, "trees": trees, "S": S, "F": F, "absence_holds": a},
                       {"species": "b (each species)", "Y": Y, "trees": trees, "S": S, "F": F, "absence_holds": b}]
rd = pd.DataFrame(rd)
print("\nReadings of the criterion (a or b, y1 or y2, trees, S1 to S3, F1 or F2):")
print(rd.to_string(index=False))
print("Readings where the absence holds:", int(rd.absence_holds.sum()), "of", len(rd))
print(rd.groupby("F").absence_holds.agg(["sum", "count"]).to_string())
print("\nRange of the season term p per trait and kind of F test, over all readings:")
print(s6.groupby(["trait", "F"]).season_p.agg(["min", "max"]).round(4).to_string())
print("\nCampaign means (monocultures and mixtures, all years):")
print(ca.groupby(["season"])[LEAF_C].mean().round(3).to_string())
# Supplemental Table S6, F values of S, W x S, MR x S and W x MR x S
TABLE_S6 = {"Starch": [2.64, 0.05, 0.93, 0.24], "Sugar": [1.56, 0.04, 2.39, 0.64], "Photo": [2.03, 0.22, 0.79, 0.09]}
s6["F_paper"] = s6.trait.map(lambda t: TABLE_S6[t][0])
cmp_ = s6.assign(maxdiff=s6.apply(lambda r: max(abs(a - b) for a, b in zip([r.season_F, r.WS_F, r.MS_F, r.WMS_F], TABLE_S6[r.trait])), axis=1))
print("\nThe four season terms against Table S6, per trait and group: the 10 closest of", len(cmp_), "fits")
print(cmp_.sort_values("maxdiff").head(10)[["trait", "Y", "trees", "species", "S", "F", "season_F", "WS_F", "MS_F", "WMS_F", "maxdiff"]].round(3).to_string(index=False))
whole = cmp_.groupby(["Y", "trees", "species", "S", "F"]).maxdiff.max().sort_values()
print("\nLargest difference in F over all three traits and four terms, per reading (the 10 closest of", len(whole), "):")
print(whole.head(10).round(3).to_string())
print("Table S6:", TABLE_S6)

Table S6: {'Starch': [2.64, 0.05, 0.93, 0.24], 'Sugar': [1.56, 0.04, 2.39, 0.64], 'Photo': [2.03, 0.22, 0.79, 0.09]}

Result: blocked (methods underspecified). With sequential tests (F1), at least one of starch, sugar and Asat has a season term with p <= 0.05 under all 24 readings (Asat p <= 0.043 under every one). With marginal tests (F2), all three have p > 0.05 under 3 of the 24 readings, 2020 and 2021 (y2) on monocultures with both species pooled, under S1, S2 and S3 (starch p 0.12 to 0.26, sugar 0.17 to 0.32, Asat 0.070 to 0.12). The criterion now names F1 and F2 as readings, and they differ; the first run, which read the tests as sequential, found the claim not reproduced. Set against the paper's F values for the four season terms, the closest readings are y2 on monocultures with both species pooled and marginal tests, under S2 and S1 (largest difference over the 12 F values 0.99 and 1.01), the readings under which the absence holds; no reading matches exactly, and with S3 the same reading differs by up to 3.1. This points to the reading but does not settle it, so the claim stays blocked.

Interaction tests and biodiversity effects (Supplemental Tables S2 and S3, Figure 5)¶

Interaction test of the claims file: per species, monoculture and mixture trees, square root, lme(trait ~ (W + MR + year + I)^3, random = ~1|Chamber), and the mixture minus monoculture contrast within each treatment and year (I1) or within each treatment averaged over 2020 and 2021 (I2), a single contrast each (Wald z). The claims file does not say over which family these contrasts are adjusted (asked in questions/dryad-4j0zpc8hz.yaml); both are run as readings: A1, each contrast alone (unadjusted), and A2, single-step over the model's eight treatment and year contrasts (I1) or its four treatment contrasts (I2), as the treatment test adjusts over the six pairs of a year. Years: Y1 (bud swelling and development duration on 2020 and 2021, the other traits on 2019 to 2021), which the criteria use, and Y2 (2020 and 2021 for all traits), reported as a variant. S1, S2 and S3 where values are negative, L1 and L2 for starch, sugar and Asat. In the tables, raw_mix_minus_mono is the difference of the raw means of the rows the model uses (under S3, without the dropped rows), and raw_all the same over all rows of the trait.

Biodiversity effects: NBE, complementarity (CE) and selection (SE) effects per mixture tree by the claims file's Eqs. 2 to 4, against the mean of the monoculture trees of the species, treatment and year (B1) or of the monoculture tree or trees of the species in the tree's own chamber (B2; every chamber has one or two, and two are averaged), on yearly tree means (L1) or per campaign (L2), each tested against zero by a one-sample t-test per species, treatment and year. With Eq. 3 as printed, CE = 2 x NBE for every tree, so its t-test is the NBE's. Under B1 the reference is the same for every tree of a group, so SE sums to zero and its mean is 0 (t = 0, p = 1) by construction. The legends' Tukey's HSD against zero is not run: it is not defined for a comparison of one group with zero.

In [31]:
PHENO = ["Flush_S1", "Dev_time", "Senes_10", "Senes_time", "Growth_length"]
# Years of the interaction model (claims file, opening comment): Y1, bud swelling and development duration on
# 2020 and 2021 and the other traits on 2019 to 2021, used by the criteria; Y2, 2020 and 2021 for all (variant).
IYEARS = {"Y1": lambda trait: [2020, 2021] if trait in ("Flush_S1", "Dev_time") else [2019, 2020, 2021],
          "Y2": lambda trait: [2020, 2021]}

# Adjustment of the mixture against monoculture contrasts, not stated in the claims file: A1 each contrast
# alone, A2 single-step over the model's eight treatment and year contrasts (I1) or four treatment contrasts (I2).
AA = ("A1", "A2")

def ireadings(trait):
    Ls = ("L1", "L2") if trait in LEAF_C else ("L1",)
    return [(Y, S, L, A) for Y in IYEARS for S in SS for L in Ls for A in AA]

IT = {}
t0 = time.time()
for sp in "BO":
    for trait in PHENO + LEAF_C + GROWTH:
        for Y, years_of in IYEARS.items():
            for L, data in (("L1", ty), ("L2", ca)):
                if L == "L2" and trait not in LEAF_C:
                    continue
                for S in SS:
                    r = o.interaction_test(data, sp, trait, years_of(trait), S)
                    IT[(sp, trait, Y, S, L)] = r
print(f"{len(IT)} interaction fits in {time.time() - t0:.0f} s; not converged:", sum(not r["converged"] for r in IT.values()))
print("Rows per fit, Y1 and Y2, S1 (S3 drops the negative values):")
print(pd.DataFrame([{"species": k[0], "trait": k[1], "Y": k[2], "S": k[3], "L": k[4], "n": r["n"], "transform": r["transform"]}
                    for k, r in IT.items() if k[3] in ("S1", "S3")]).pivot_table(index=["species", "trait", "L"], columns=["Y", "S"], values="n").to_string())

def itable(sp, traits, treat, years=("2020", "2021", "2020-21")):
    rows = []
    for trait in traits:
        for reading in ireadings(trait):
            fit = IT[(sp, trait, *reading[:3])]
            c = fit["contrasts"]
            for _, r in c[(c.treat == treat) & c.year.isin(years)].iterrows():
                rows.append({"species": sp, "trait": trait, "reading": "/".join(reading), "Y": reading[0], "A": reading[3], "treat": treat,
                             "I": "I2" if r.year == "2020-21" else "I1", "year": r.year, "transform": fit["transform"],
                             "raw_mix_minus_mono": r.raw_mix_minus_mono, "raw_all": r.raw_all, "estimate": r.estimate, "z": r.z,
                             "p": r.p if reading[3] == "A1" else r.p_family})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

BE = {}
for trait in PHENO + LEAF_C + GROWTH:
    for B in ("B1", "B2"):
        for L, data in (("L1", ty), ("L2", ca)):
            if L == "L2" and trait not in LEAF_C:
                continue
            eff = o.biodiversity_effects(data[data.Year.isin([2020, 2021])], trait, B, by_campaign=(L == "L2"))
            BE[(trait, B, L)] = o.t_tests(eff)

def absence_combos(sp, treat, nbe_years):
    """Per reading (Y, S, L, I, B): significant mixture-monoculture contrasts and NBEs in the years judged."""
    t = itable(sp, LEAF_C + GROWTH, treat)
    rows = []
    b_all = btable(sp, LEAF_C + GROWTH, treat, years=nbe_years, effects=("NBE",))
    for Y in IYEARS:
        for S in SS:
            for L in ("L1", "L2"):
                for A in AA:
                    for I in ("I1", "I2"):
                        g = t[(t.I == I) & t.apply(lambda r: r.reading == (f"{Y}/{S}/{L}/{A}" if r.trait in LEAF_C else f"{Y}/{S}/L1/{A}"), axis=1)]
                        nc = int((g.p < 0.05).sum())
                        for B in ("B1", "B2"):
                            b = b_all[(b_all.B == B) & ((b_all.L == L) | ~b_all.trait.isin(LEAF_C))]
                            nb_ = int((b.p < 0.05).sum())
                            rows.append({"Y": Y, "S": S, "L": L, "A": A, "I": I, "B": B, "contrasts_tested": len(g), "contrasts_significant": nc,
                                         "NBE_tested": len(b), "NBE_significant": nb_, "absence_holds": nc == 0 and nb_ == 0})
    return pd.DataFrame(rows)

def btable(sp, traits, treat, years=(2020, 2021), effects=("NBE", "CE", "SE")):
    rows = []
    for trait in traits:
        for B in ("B1", "B2"):
            for L in (("L1", "L2") if trait in LEAF_C else ("L1",)):
                t = BE[(trait, B, L)]
                t = t[(t.Species == sp) & (t.treat == treat) & t.Year.isin(years) & t.effect.isin(effects)]
                rows.append(t.assign(trait=trait, B=B, L=L))
    return pd.concat(rows)[["Species", "trait", "treat", "Year", "B", "L", "effect", "n", "mean", "t", "p"]]

tables2-phenology-no-interaction¶

Species mixture did not change phenology in either species, except a development duration about 2 days shorter in oak mixtures than monocultures under lower moisture, whose net biodiversity effect was not significant.

    for I in ("I1", "I2"):

        target = s[(s.species == "O") & (s.trait == "Dev_time") & (s.treat == "MR")]

Result: not reproduced. Under Y1, the oak development duration contrast under MR is not significant (raw mixture minus monoculture -0.83 days in 2020, -1.67 in 2021, -1.25 over both years, p 0.14 to 0.67 each alone (A1), 0.63 to 1.0 adjusted over the model's contrasts (A2)), and other phenology contrasts are: under A1, 6 per reading under I1 and 2 under I2, and under A2, 2 under I1 and 2 under I2 (for example oak development duration under C in 2021, +5.5 days, p 0.0018, and oak onset of senescence under W in 2020, +18.8 days, p 0.0008). The Y2 variant gives the same outcome under A1 and A2. The NBE of oak development duration under MR does not differ from zero (p 0.055 to 0.52), as the claim says.

tables3-beech-warming-no-interaction¶

Under warming, species mixture did not change beech leaf-level carbon relations or growth in 2020 and 2021, and beech showed no net biodiversity effect on these traits in 2021.

In [33]:
t = itable("B", LEAF_C + GROWTH, "W")
print("Beech under W, mixture minus monoculture; significant contrasts:", int((t.p < 0.05).sum()), "of", len(t))
print(t[t.p < 0.05].round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("Significant per reading and I:"); print(t.groupby(["reading", "I"]).apply(lambda g: int((g.p < 0.05).sum())).to_string())
b = btable("B", LEAF_C + GROWTH, "W", years=(2020, 2021), effects=("NBE",))
print("\nNBE, beech under W:")
print(b.round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("2021 NBEs with p < 0.05:", int(((b.Year == 2021) & (b.p < 0.05)).sum()), "; 2020 (reported, not judged):", int(((b.Year == 2020) & (b.p < 0.05)).sum()))
combos = absence_combos("B", "W", (2021,))
print("\nPer reading (contrasts 2020 and 2021, NBE 2021):")
print(combos.to_string(index=False))

Under Y1 the absence holds under 3 of 24 readings with each contrast alone (A1: I2 with B2 on yearly means, under S1, S2 and S3) and 6 of 24 with contrasts adjusted over the model's contrasts (A2: B2 on yearly means, I1 or I2); under the others a mixture against monoculture contrast is significant (beech starch in 2020, mixtures higher by 3.6 %, p 0.018 to 0.045 under I1 and A1, none under A2), or a 2021 NBE differs from zero (Asat, -60 to -67, p 0.0002 to 0.019, except B2 on yearly means, p 0.078). The Y2 variant gives 2 (A1) and 6 (A2) of 24.

figs2-beech-asat-selection-warming¶

Under warming, beech Asat showed a positive selection effect, too weak to change the net biodiversity effect.

In [34]:
b = btable("B", ["Photo"], "W")
print(b.round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for (B, L), g in b.groupby(["B", "L"]):
    hold = []
    for yr in (2020, 2021):
        se = g[(g.Year == yr) & (g.effect == "SE")].iloc[0]
        nbe = g[(g.Year == yr) & (g.effect == "NBE")].iloc[0]
        hold.append(bool(se["mean"] > 0 and se.p < 0.05 and not nbe.p < 0.05))
    rows.append({"B": B, "L": L, "holds_2020": hold[0], "holds_2021": hold[1], "reproduced": any(hold)})
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).to_string(index=False))

Result: not reproduced. Under no reading and in neither year is the selection effect on beech Asat under W positive and significant: its mean is 0 under B1 on yearly means (by construction), 11.6 and 3.3 (p 0.81 and 0.81) under B1 on campaigns, and -0.3 to -19 (p 0.32 to 1.0) under B2. tables3-oak-warming-diameter¶

Under warming, oak in mixtures had a diameter increment about 0.3 cm per year lower than in monocultures, and no other difference between mixtures and monocultures.

In [35]:
t = itable("O", LEAF_C + GROWTH, "W")
print("Oak under W, diameter increment:")
print(t[t.trait == "Diameter_growth"].round(4).to_string(index=False))
print("\nOther oak traits under W with p < 0.05:")
print(t[(t.trait != "Diameter_growth") & (t.p < 0.05)].round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for reading in ireadings("Diameter_growth"):
    for I in ("I1", "I2"):
        g = t[(t.reading == "/".join(reading)) & (t.I == I)]
        dia = g[g.trait == "Diameter_growth"]
        hits = dia[(dia.p < 0.05) & (dia.raw_mix_minus_mono < 0)]
        u1 = bool(hits.raw_mix_minus_mono.map(lambda v: within(-v, 0.3, 0.03)).any())
        u2 = bool(hits.raw_mix_minus_mono.map(lambda v: within(-v, 0.03, 0.003)).any())
        others = int((g[g.trait != "Diameter_growth"].p < 0.05).sum())
        rows.append({"reading": "/".join(reading), "Y": reading[0], "I": I, "diameter lower and significant": len(hits) > 0,
                     "within 0.3 (u1)": u1, "within 0.03 (u2, mm in cm)": u2, "other significant": others})
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).to_string(index=False))

Result: not reproduced. Under MR, beech diameter increment is not significantly lower in mixtures under any reading, A1 or A2: the 2020 difference is -0.20 (p 0.46 to 0.58 under A1); in 2021 mixtures grow more under S1 and S2 (+0.67, p 0.018 to 0.082 under A1, 0.13 to 0.47 under A2), and on the rows S3 keeps the difference is -0.17 (p 0.65 and 0.82 under A1); over both years it is +0.22 (S1, S2, p 0.28 to 0.53 under A1) or -0.22 (S3, p 0.87 and 0.93 under A1).

fig5-beech-sugar-nbe-mr¶

Under low soil moisture, beech showed a negative net biodiversity effect on sugar concentration, driven by a negative complementarity effect.

In [38]:
b = btable("B", ["Sugar"], "MR")
print(b.round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for (B, L), g in b.groupby(["B", "L"]):
    for yr in (2020, 2021):
        h = g[g.Year == yr].set_index("effect")
        rows.append({"B": B, "L": L, "year": yr,
                     "NBE<0 sig": bool(h.loc["NBE", "mean"] < 0 and h.loc["NBE", "p"] < 0.05),
                     "CE<0 sig": bool(h.loc["CE", "mean"] < 0 and h.loc["CE", "p"] < 0.05)})
r = pd.DataFrame(rows); r["both"] = r["NBE<0 sig"] & r["CE<0 sig"]
print(r.to_string(index=False))
print(r.groupby(["B", "L"]).both.any().rename("reproduced (2020 or 2021)").to_string())

Result: reproduced. In 2020, the NBE on beech sugar under MR is negative and differs from zero under all four readings (-94 to -95, p 0.0025 to 0.036), and so does the complementarity effect, which is twice the NBE (Eq. 3 as printed). In 2021 it is near zero (p >= 0.71). The year found is 2020, as the Discussion says; the sign of CE is negative, as the Results say. With Eq. 3 read as in the claims file, CE is twice the NBE for every tree, so the complementarity part is not tested apart from the NBE; a question asks whether this is the paper's computation.

oak-mr-no-interaction¶

Under low soil moisture alone, oak showed no effect of species interactions and no net biodiversity effect.

In [39]:
t = itable("O", LEAF_C + GROWTH, "MR")
print("Oak under MR, mixture minus monoculture; significant:", int((t.p < 0.05).sum()), "of", len(t))

tables3-beech-wmr-height¶

Under combined warming and lower moisture, beech height increment was about 2 cm per year larger in monocultures than in mixtures.

t = itable("B", ["Height_growth"], "WMR")

print(t.round(4).to_string(index=False))

tables3-oak-wmr-diameter¶

Under combined warming and lower moisture, oak diameter increment was 1.4 cm larger in mixtures than in monocultures.

In [41]:
t = itable("O", ["Diameter_growth"], "WMR")
t["sig_larger"] = (t.p < 0.05) & (t.estimate > 0)
t["u1_within_1.4"] = t.raw_mix_minus_mono.map(lambda v: within(v, 1.4, 0.14))
t["u2_within_0.14"] = t.raw_mix_minus_mono.map(lambda v: within(v, 0.14, 0.014))
t["all_u1_within"] = t.raw_all.map(lambda v: within(v, 1.4, 0.14))
t["all_u2_within"] = t.raw_all.map(lambda v: within(v, 0.14, 0.014))
t["holds"] = t.sig_larger & (t["u1_within_1.4"] | t["u2_within_0.14"])
t["holds_on_all_rows"] = t.sig_larger & (t.all_u1_within | t.all_u2_within)
print(t.round(4).to_string(index=False))
print(t.groupby(["reading"])[["holds", "holds_on_all_rows"]].any().to_string())

Result: blocked (methods underspecified). Under WMR, oak diameter increment is significantly larger in mixtures under every Y1 reading in 2021 when each contrast is taken alone (A1), but the difference is within 1.4 +- 0.14 only under Y1 with S3, and only on the tree-years the S3 model keeps (+1.38, p 0.023). Over all trees the 2021 difference is +1.69 under every reading, outside tolerance under both unit readings. Adjusted over the model's eight treatment and year contrasts (A2), the same S3 contrast has p 0.16, while under S1 and S2 the 2021 contrast stays significant (p 0.0017 to 0.0096) at +1.69. Under the Y2 variant with S3 the 2021 difference of 1.38 has p 0.051 (A1). The claim holds only for one choice of two details the claims file does not fix (the family of the contrasts, and the rows over which the difference is taken under S3), so it is blocked until the question on them is answered.

fig5-oak-asat-nbe-wmr-2021¶

Under combined warming and drier soils in 2021, oak showed a positive net biodiversity effect on Asat, explained by complementarity.

In [42]:
b = btable("O", ["Photo"], "WMR", years=(2020, 2021))
print(b.round(4).to_string(index=False))
rows = []
for (B, L), g in b[b.Year == 2021].groupby(["B", "L"]):
    h = g.set_index("effect")
    nbe = bool(h.loc["NBE", "mean"] > 0 and h.loc["NBE", "p"] < 0.05)
    ce_sig = bool(h.loc["CE", "mean"] > 0 and h.loc["CE", "p"] < 0.05)
    out = "reproduced" if nbe and ce_sig else "partially_reproduced" if nbe and h.loc["CE", "mean"] > 0 else "not_reproduced"
    rows.append({"B": B, "L": L, "NBE>0 sig": nbe, "CE>0 sig": ce_sig, "outcome": out})
print(pd.DataFrame(rows).to_string(index=False))

Result: reproduced. In 2021 the NBE on oak Asat under WMR is positive and differs from zero under all four readings (+193 to +194, p 0.0003 to 0.028), and so does the complementarity effect (twice the NBE). With Eq. 3 read as in the claims file, CE is twice the NBE for every tree, so the complementarity part is not tested apart from the NBE; a question asks whether this is the paper's computation.

Blocked claims: what the dataset holds¶

figs1-phenology-no-moisture, methods-treatment-magnitudes, fig6-soil-moisture-mr-equals-wmr, tables5-annual-climate and tables4-initial-sizes-similar are blocked: the dataset has no soil moisture, no chamber climate records and no initial sizes. The only air temperatures are the treatment means per year and season printed below, from which part of two claims can be compared with the paper; these comparisons decide no outcome.

A failure to reproduce here may be a failure of this attempt, not of the paper: see the reasoning and the differences from the paper's method.

How the analysis differs from the paper's. The phenology regressions use the treatment's temperature for each tree (the dataset has no chamber value) from an unnamed sensor. GDD and chilling days are the deposited values. The mixed models are fitted in Python (statsmodels, REML), with nlme's tests and multcomp-style single-step contrasts computed here; the paper used R. Exact duplicate rows are counted once and one tree's two different rows for one campaign are averaged. Where the claims file leaves a detail open, every reading is run, and a claim whose readings disagree is blocked. This version follows the answers to the three questions of the first run: S3 (negative values dropped) is a reading for starch, sugar, Asat and the increments, the interaction model uses the years of Y1 (Y2 as a variant), and Table S6 is read under y1 and y2 with sequential and marginal F tests. After the first verification round, the mixture against monoculture contrasts are run both alone (A1) and adjusted over the model's contrasts (A2), as the claims file does not name the family; this, and the rows over which the raw difference is taken under S3, decide tables3-oak-wmr-diameter, which is now blocked. Two variants outside the claims file are shown: P1 with a random intercept per treatment and year, under which fig1cd-development-duration (oak p 0.11) and fig1-oak-steeper-advance (p 0.097) would not hold, and the Table S6 F values against the paper's. Three questions on these points are in questions/dryad-4j0zpc8hz.yaml.

What was found about the dataset. The treatment codes in the data (C, D, H, HD) are not those of the metadata (C, W, MR, WMR); the Warming and Soil moisture reduction columns give the mapping. 35 rows are exact duplicates and one tree has two different rows for one campaign. Starch, sugar and Asat have negative values. Growth_length is not onset of senescence minus bud swelling, as the Methods define it, and neither the workbook nor Metadata.csv defines it. Diameter increments are labelled cm but are consistent with mm. Four compartments hold a monoculture tree of one species and a mixture tree of the other. Soil moisture, the chamber climate records and the initial sizes, on which five claims rest, are not deposited. Refitting the treatment model with marginal F tests reproduces Table S1 exactly for oak AGWB increment only; oak midday water potential, beech AGWB increment and beech Asat are within 0.02, 0.21 and 0.25.
